Threat timeline: Chlorine (i81-md-pa-line-night)

What the modelled cloud reaches and when, in time order, from the engine's held record. Each row is one place reaching one health-effect level. Times are a model's. T55

  • Engine: SCICHEM, record db0646724643
  • Wind: the forecast, hour by hour, from 02:00 (350 degrees, 7.4 km/h at the release)
  • Release assumed to end at 02:07 (H+7 min); who can confirm: the incident commander
  • The last level leaves the scene at 02:50 (H+50 min)
  • The brief's time is the release time, 02:00; lead is the time from it
  • Residents: 2020 Census DHC by block, each block counted by the share of its area inside a level's area reached at any time; workers: LODES jobs by workplace block, shown on work-hours rows
  • Class F: the moving cloud is drawn hour by hour by level at a 1.25 m vertical setting, an approximation that is not converged; each level's planning area is its area, filled, together with SLAB's extent check, dashed, on one wind with no times; every time on this brief is from that run, and an arrival may be earlier than shown by up to 12 minutes (6 in the first hour; against the finest run held, docs/evidence/hb192/arrival_sign.tsv) (Conditions of use, section T58)
  • The held evaluations of stable dense releases reach 5 km at most, for either engine (DN-028 ADDENDUM-02 section 3 items 6 and 7); this timeline's rows reach 38.9 km, where a shape is a model's extrapolation
  • Inside SLAB's extent check at a level, with no time from the moving cloud there: 2 rows

Exercise events list (CSV, HSEEP MSEL columns) The columns are the HSEEP doctrine's MSEL entry (2020, p. 46); hazbrief fills the event number, time and message from this timeline and leaves the planner's columns empty.

The first minutes, from the held sources (hazbrief sends nothing and drafts no message)

one organization must be assigned the responsibility of alerting the public as soon as word of the accidental release is received. Delay in alerting the public can lead to the loss of life. NRT-1, Hazardous Materials Emergency Planning Guide (2001), p. 63
The SIP warning should be disseminated without delay using a variety of means- reverse 911 on landlines, text messages to cell phones in the area, PA broadcast, police car PA systems, etc. LLNL mass casualty framework (2016), p. 25
A NWEM is a nonscheduled product and will be relayed at the request of alerting authorities over NWS dissemination systems. NWS Directive 10-518, p. 12
Provisions must be made for quickly moving traffic out of the risk zone and also for preventing outside traffic from entering the risk zone. NRT-1, Hazardous Materials Emergency Planning Guide (2001), p. 66

Time to act, from the held sources (no row is marked against them; the lead time is the signal)

Several analyses suggest that it will take 5 to 10 minutes on average to implement shelter-in-place, once a sheltering decision is made by a household. Sorensen, Shumpert and Vogt (2002), ORNL/TM-2002/144, p. 22
a considerable amount of time (up to one to two hours using conventional warning practices) may elapse before most people in the affected area hear, absorb, and decide to respond to the instructions Sorensen, Shumpert and Vogt (2002), ORNL/TM-2002/144, p. 21
For example, one might shelter with an expected plume duration of less than 30 minutes and evacuate with an expected plume duration of over 120 minutes. The middle ground of 30 to 120 minutes is a 'gray area' where the decision outcome is unclear. Sorensen, Shumpert and Vogt (2002), ORNL/TM-2002/144, p. 25
